We now expect a fairly high degree of accuracy â the more important differentiators are how well a model fits within a productâs infrastructure and what developers do with its predictions.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This week, weâre introducing &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/#segment\&quot;&gt;background removal for Images&lt;/a&gt;. This feature runs a dichotomous image segmentation model on Workers AI to isolate subjects in an image from their backgrounds. We took a controlled, deliberate approach to testing models for efficiency and accuracy.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Hereâs how we evaluated various image segmentation models to develop background removal.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;a-primer-on-image-segmentation\&quot;&gt;A primer on image segmentation&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#a-primer-on-image-segmentation\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;In computer vision, image segmentation is the process of splitting an image into meaningful parts.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Segmentation models produce a mask that assigns each pixel to a specific category. This differs from detection models, which donât classify every pixel but instead mark regions of interest. A face detection model, such as the one that informs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/#gravity\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;face cropping&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, draws bounding boxes based on where it thinks there are faces. (If youâre curious, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-face-cropping-for-images/#from-pixels-to-people\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;our post on face cropping&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; discusses how we use these bounding boxes to perform crop and zoom operations.)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Salient object detection is a type of segmentation that highlights the parts of an image that most stand out. Most salient detection models create a binary mask that categorizes the most prominent (or salient) pixels as the âforegroundâ and all other pixels as the âbackgroundâ. In contrast, a multi-class mask considers the broader context and labels each pixel as one of several possible classes, like âdogâ or âchairâ. These multi-class masks are the basis of content analysis models, which distinguish which pixels belong to specific objects or types of objects.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/qV2QVZYEdqdigCTuqBuHu/cf4873dddf3b30503aac6643ded1a5ab/image3.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1118\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;In this photograph of my dog, a detection model predicts that a bounding box contains a dog; a segmentation model predicts that some pixels belong to a dog, while all other pixels donât.&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For our use case, we needed a model that could produce a soft saliency mask, which predicts how strongly each pixel belongs to either the foreground (objects of interest) or the background. That is, each pixel is assigned a value on a scale of 0â255, where 0 is completely transparent and 255 is fully opaque. Most background pixels are labeled at (or near) 0; foreground pixels may vary in opacity, depending on its degree of saliency.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In principle, a background removal feature must be able to accurately predict saliency across a broad range of contexts. For example, e-commerce and retail vendors want to display all products on a uniform, white background; in creative and image editing applications, developers want to enable users to create stickers and cutouts from uploaded content, including images of people or avatars.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In our research, we focused primarily on the following four image segmentation models:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.09007\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;U&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;-Net (U Square Net)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;: Trained on the largest saliency dataset (&lt;a href=\&quot;https://saliencydetection.net/duts/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;DUST-TR&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;) of 10,553 images, which were then horizontally flipped to reach a total of 21,106 training images.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.03041\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;IS-Net (Intermediate Supervision Network)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;: A novel, two-step approach from the same authors of U2-Net; this model produces cleaner boundaries for images with noisy, cluttered backgrounds.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.03407\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;BiRefNet (Bilateral Reference Network)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;: Specifically designed to segment complex and high-resolution images with accuracy by checking that the small details match the big picture.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.02643\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;SAM (Segment Anything Model)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;: Developed by Meta to allow segmentation by providing prompts and input points.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Different scales of information allow computational models to build a holistic view of an image. Global context considers the overall shape of objects and how areas of pixels relate to the entire image, while local context traces fine details like edges, corners, and textures. If local context focuses on the trees and their leaves, then global context represents the entire forest.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/xuebinqin/U-2-Net\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;U&lt;/u&gt;&lt;u&gt;&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/u&gt;&lt;u&gt;-Net&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; extracts information using a multi-scale approach, where it analyzes an image at different zoom levels, then combines its predictions in a single step. The model analyzes global and local context at the same time, so it works well on images with multiple objects of varying sizes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/xuebinqin/DIS\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;IS-Net&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; introduces a new, two-step strategy called intermediate supervision. First, the model separates the foreground from the background, identifying potential areas that likely belong to objects of interest â all other pixels are labeled as the background. Second, it refines the boundaries of the highlighted objects to produce a final pixel-level mask.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The initial suppression of the background results in cleaner, more precise edges, as the segmentation focuses only on the highlighted objects of interest and is less likely to mistakenly include background pixels in the final mask. This model especially excels when dealing with complex images with cluttered backgrounds.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Both models output their predictions in a single direction for scale. U&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;-Net interprets the global and local context in one pass, while Is-Net begins with the global context, then focuses on the local context.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In contrast, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/ZhengPeng7/BiRefNet\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;BiRefNet&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; refines its predictions over multiple passes, moving in both contextual directions. Like Is-Net, it initially creates a map that roughly highlights the salient object, then traces the finer details. However, BiRefNet moves from global to local context, then from local context back to global. In other words, after refining the edges of the object, it feeds the output back to the large-scale view. This way, the model can check that the small-scale details align with the broader image structure, providing higher accuracy on high-resolution images.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;U&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;-Net, IS-Net, and BiRefNet are exclusively saliency detection models, producing masks that distinguish foreground pixels from background pixels. However, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/facebookresearch/segment-anything\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;SAM&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; was designed to be more extensible and general; its primary goal is to segment any object based on specified inputs, not only salient objects. This means that the model can also be used to create multi-class masks that label various objects within an image, even if they arenât the primary focus of an image.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;how-we-measure-segmentation-accuracy\&quot;&gt;How we measure segmentation accuracy&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-we-measure-segmentation-accuracy\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;In most saliency datasets, the actual location of the object is known as the ground-truth area. These regions are typically defined by human annotators, who manually trace objects of interest in each image. This provides a reliable reference to evaluate model predictions.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6wAV8lQcsZHosKoFyEIce1/495b3d70960027b795ec1a62f2d46a59/BLOG-2928_3.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;Our tests compared the predicted area to the ground-truth area, or the true boundaries of the object.\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;895\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;Photograph by &lt;/sub&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.linkedin.com/in/fang-allen\&quot;&gt;&lt;sub&gt;&lt;u&gt;Allen Fang&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each model outputs a predicted area (where it thinks the foreground pixels are), which can be compared against the ground-truth area (where the foreground pixels actually are).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Models are evaluated for segmentation accuracy based on common metrics like Intersection over Union, Dice coefficient, and pixel accuracy. Each score takes a slightly different approach to quantify the alignment between the predicted and ground-truth areas (âPâ and âGâ, respectively, in the formulas below).&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;intersection-over-union\&quot;&gt;Intersection over Union&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#intersection-over-union\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Intersection over Union (IoU), also called the Jaccard index, measures how well the predicted area matches the true object. That is, it counts the number of foreground pixels that are shared in both the predicted and ground-truth masks. Mathematically, IoU is written as:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6zVQSLlKaFuVUQrDcAlf0Y/4254010745caf0d207d8f8e8181f4c9c/BLOG-2928_4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;448\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;Jaccard formula&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The formula divides the intersection (Pâ©G), or the pixels where the predicted and ground-truth areas overlap, by the union (PâªG), or the total area of pixels that belong to either area, counting the overlapping pixels only once.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/7KFLB15btpCQuKuTqakBjp/91e78ec6d565e3723c5d76b3a65a441d/unnamed__23_.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 5\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;895\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;IoU produces a score between 0 and 1. A higher value indicates a closer overlap between the predicted and ground-truth areas. A perfect match, although rare, would score 1, while a smaller overlapping area brings the score closer to 0.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/oe82x3rPo8XoNnwG3KBRy/22f591adb6ab27b3ad05f91b13eddff7/BLOG-2928_6.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 6\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;448\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;dice-coefficient\&quot;&gt;Dice coefficient&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#dice-coefficient\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Dice coefficient, also called the SÃ¸rensenâDice index, similarly compares how well the modelâs prediction matches reality, but is much more forgiving than the IoU score. It gives more weight to the shared pixels between the predicted and actual foreground, even if the areas differ in size. Mathematically, the Dice coefficient is written as:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4UiJUJrjagwkmQNvdkiPC3/e17eaa8f22f57114a91f1e58fc3a76fb/BLOG-2928_7.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 7\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;448\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;SÃ¸rensenâDice formula&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The formula divides twice the intersection (Pâ©G) by the sum of pixels in both predicted and ground-truth areas (P+G), counting any overlapping pixels twice.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/7vcFBAoRJ9wpyAt8m4Sn7x/8b1962de717701ff348e90ec8b86286e/BLOG-2928_8.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 8\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;895\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Like IoU, the Dice coefficient also produces a value between 0 and 1, indicating a more accurate match as it approaches 1.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;pixel-accuracy\&quot;&gt;Pixel accuracy&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#pixel-accuracy\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Pixel accuracy measures the percentage of pixels that were correctly labeled as either the foreground or the background. Mathematically, pixel accuracy is written as:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/40HkiVe1a2i1dSguDk1TxO/990e49cd4d40a4eaa29078948bc9d7e8/unnamed__24_.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;448\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;Pixel accuracy formula&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The formula divides the number of correctly predicted pixels by the total number of pixels in the image.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1GX83EmXBSLhGlHvGFLqnn/f65fbd110f4b1d201f7585723ced0f34/image10.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 10\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1118\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;The total area of correctly predicted pixels is the sum of foreground and background pixels that accurately match the ground-truth areas.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The correctly predicted foreground is the intersection of the predicted and ground-truth areas (Pâ©G). The inverse of the predicted area (Pâ, or 1âP) represents the pixels that the model identifies as the background; the inverse of the ground-truth area (Gâ, or 1âG) represents the actual boundaries of the background. When these two inverted areas overlap (Pââ©Gâ, or (1âP)â©(1âG)), this intersection is the correctly predicted background.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;interpreting-the-metrics\&quot;&gt;Interpreting the metrics&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#interpreting-the-metrics\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Of the three metrics, IoU is the most conservative measure of segmentation accuracy. Small mistakes, such as including extra background pixels in the predicted foreground, reduce the score noticeably. This metric is most valuable for applications that require precise boundaries, such as autonomous driving systems.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Meanwhile, the Dice coefficient rewards the overlapping pixels more heavily, and subsequently tends to be higher than the IoU score for the same prediction. In model evaluations, this metric is favored over IoU when itâs more important to capture the object than to penalize mistakes. For example, in medical imaging, the risk of missing a true positive substantially outweighs the inconvenience of flagging a false positive.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In the context of background removal, we biased toward the IoU score and Dice coefficient over pixel accuracy. Pixel accuracy can be misleading, especially when processing an image where background pixels comprise the majority of pixels.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/7K8TWmRLdJNIza43UoXhD8/c9a42ed7074ce975afd8f7e783db5849/BLOG-2928_11.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 11\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;560\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;For example, consider an image with 900 background pixels and 100 foreground pixels. A model that correctly predicts only 5 foreground pixels â 5% of all foreground pixels â will score deceptively high in pixel accuracy. Intuitively, weâd likely say that this model performed poorly. However, assuming all 900 background pixels were correctly predicted, the model maintains 90.5% pixel accuracy, despite missing the subject almost entirely.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;pixels-predictions-and-patterns\&quot;&gt;Pixels, predictions, and patterns&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#pixels-predictions-and-patterns\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;To determine the most suitable model for the Images API, we performed a series of tests using the open-source &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/danielgatis/rembg\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;rembg&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; library, which combines all relevant models in a single interface.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each model was tasked with outputting a prediction mask to label foreground versus background pixels. We pulled images from two saliency datasets: &lt;a href=\&quot;https://huggingface.co/datasets/schirrmacher/humans\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Humans&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt; contains over 7,000 images of people with varying skin tones, clothing, and hairstyles, while &lt;a href=\&quot;https://xuebinqin.github.io/dis/index.html#overview\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;DIS5K&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (version 1.5) spans a vast range of objects and scenes. If a model contained variants that were pre-trained on specific types of segmentation (e.g. clothes, humans), then we repeated the tests for the generalized model and each variant.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our experiments were executed on a GPU with 23 GB VRAM to mirror realistic hardware constraints, similar to the environment where we already run a face detection model. We also replicated the same tests on a larger GPU instance with 94 GB VRAM; this served as an upper-bound reference point to benchmark potential speed gains if additional compute were available. Cloudflare typically reserves larger GPUs for more compute-intensive &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/models/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI workloads&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; â we viewed these tests more as an exploration for comparison than as a production scenario.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;During our analysis, we started to see key trends emerge:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;On the smaller GPU, inference times were generally faster for lightweight models like U&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;-Net (176 MB) and Is-Net (179 MB). The average speed across both datasets were 307 milliseconds for U&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;-Net and 351 milliseconds for Is-Net. On the opposite end, BiRefNet (973 MB) had noticeably slower output times, averaging 821 milliseconds across its two generalized variants.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;BiRefNet ran 2.4 times faster on the larger GPU, reducing its average inference time to 351 milliseconds â comparable to the other models, despite its larger size. In contrast, the lighter models did not show any notable speed gain with additional compute, suggesting that scaling hardware configurations primarily benefits heavier models. In &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/background-removal/#appendix-1-inference-time-in-milliseconds\&quot;&gt;Appendix 1&lt;/a&gt; (âInference Time in Millisecondsâ), we compare speed across models and GPU instances.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/55Tk0RjbvoffPVQT85UJQe/ca1f2280768495f3be52425e642fdd25/BLOG-2928_12.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 12\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1118\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also observed distinct patterns when comparing model performance across the two saliency datasets. Most notably, all models ran faster on the Humans dataset, where images of people tend to be single-subject and relatively uniform. The DIS5K dataset, in contrast, includes images with higher complexity â that is, images with more objects, cluttered backgrounds, or multiple objects of varying scales.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Slower predictions suggest a relationship between visual complexity and the computation needed to identify the important parts of an image. In other words, datasets with simpler, well-separated objects can be analyzed more quickly, while complex scenes require more computation to generate accurate masks.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Similarly, complexity challenges accuracy as much as it does efficiency. In our tests, all models demonstrated higher segmentation accuracy with the Humans dataset. In &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/background-removal/#appendix-2-measures-of-model-accuracy\&quot;&gt;Appendix 2&lt;/a&gt; (âMeasures of Model Accuracyâ), we present our results for segmentation accuracy across both datasets.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Specialized variants scored slightly higher in accuracy compared to their generalized counterparts. But in broad, practical applications, selecting a specialized model for every input isnât realistic, at least for our initial beta version. We favored general-purpose models that can produce accurate predictions without prior classification. For this reason, we excluded SAM â while powerful in its intended use cases, SAM is designed to work with additional inputs. On unprompted segmentation tasks, it produced lower accuracy scores (and much higher inference times) amongst the models we tested.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;All BiRefNet variants showed greater accuracy compared to other models. The generalized variants (&lt;code&gt;-genera&lt;/code&gt;l and &lt;code&gt;-dis&lt;/code&gt;) were just as accurate as its more specialized variants like &lt;code&gt;-portrait&lt;/code&gt;. The &lt;code&gt;birefnet-general&lt;/code&gt; variant, in particular, achieved a high IoU score of 0.87 and Dice coefficient of 0.92, averaged across both datasets.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In contrast, the generalized U&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;-Net model showed high accuracy on the Humans dataset, reaching an IoU score of 0.89 and a Dice coefficient of 0.94, but received a low IoU score of 0.39 and Dice coefficient of 0.52 on the DIS5K dataset. The &lt;code&gt;isnet-general-use&lt;/code&gt; model performed substantially better, obtaining an average IoU score of 0.82 and Dice coefficient of 0.89 across both datasets.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We observed whether models could interpret both the global and local context of an image. In some scenarios, the U&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;-Net and Is-Net models captured the overall gist of an image, but couldnât accurately trace fine edges. We designed one test around measuring how well each model could isolate bicycle wheels; for variety, we included images across both interior and exterior backgrounds. Lower scoring models, while correctly labeling the area surrounding the wheel, struggled with the pixels between the thin spokes and produced prediction masks that included these background pixels.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6mzRTqXhZRk0GuzwuIRu4p/b251aa4f3dbeecc11dbba931623607e5/BLOG-2928_13.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 13\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;597\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;Photograph by &lt;/sub&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://unsplash.com/photos/person-near-bike-p6OU_gENRL0\&quot;&gt;&lt;sub&gt;&lt;u&gt;Yomex Owo on Unsplash&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;sub&gt;&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In other scenarios, the models showed the opposite limitation: they produced masks with clean edges, but failed to identify the focus of the image. We ran another test using a photograph of a gray T-shirt against black gym flooring. Both generalized U&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;-Net and Is-Net models labeled only the logo as the salient object, creating a mask that omitted the rest of the shirt entirely.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Meanwhile, the BiRefNet model achieved high accuracy across both types of tests. Its architecture passes information bidirectionally, allowing details at the pixel level to be informed by the larger scene (and vice versa). In practice, this means that BiRefNet interprets how fine-grained edges fit into the broader object. For our beta version, we opted to use the BiRefNet model to drive decisions for background removal.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/741GSfhMn8MPykb6NkWUJV/1ef5006aea8f67a4faeec73862d97ced/BLOG-2928_14.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 14\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;649\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;Unlike lower scoring models, the BiRefNet model understood that the entire shirt is the true subject of the image.&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;applying-background-removal-with-the-images-api\&quot;&gt;Applying background removal with the Images API&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#applying-background-removal-with-the-images-api\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Images API now supports &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/#segment\&quot;&gt;automatic background removal&lt;/a&gt; for &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/upload-images/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;hosted&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;remote&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; images. This feature is available in open beta to all Cloudflare users on &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/pricing/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Free and Paid plans&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/3iglNDllwEMvg6ygDvTRNc/a354422efd166cb3b48ee10995e78aa4/unnamed__25_.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 15\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;895\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Use the &lt;code&gt;segment&lt;/code&gt; parameter when optimizing an image through a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;specially-formatted Images URL&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; or a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;worker&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and Cloudflare will isolate the subject of your image and convert the background into transparent pixels. This can be combined with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;other optimization operations&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, as shown in the transformation URL below:Â &lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Rust\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Rust\&quot;&gt;example.com/cdn-cgi/image/gravity=face,zoom=0.5,segment=foreground,background=white/image.png&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;This request will:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Crop the image toward the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/#gravity\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;detected face&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Isolate the subject in the image, replacing the background with transparent pixels.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/#background\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Fill the transparent pixels&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; with a solid white color (&lt;code&gt;#FFFFFF&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can also &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/bindings/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;bind the Images API&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to your worker to build programmatic workflows that give more fine-grained control over how images will be optimized. To demonstrate how this works, I made a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://studio.yaydeanna.workers.dev/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;simple image editing app&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for creating cutouts and overlays, built entirely on Images and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. This can be used to create images &lt;a href=\&quot;https://studio.yaydeanna.workers.dev/?order=0%2C1%2C2&amp;i0=icecream&amp;vertEdge0=bottom&amp;vertVal0=0&amp;horEdge0=left&amp;h0=400&amp;bg0=1&amp;i1=pete&amp;vertEdge1=top&amp;horEdge1=left&amp;h1=700&amp;bg1=1&amp;i2=iceland&amp;vertEdge2=top&amp;horEdge2=left\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;like the one below&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Here, we apply background removal to isolate the dog and ice cream cone, then overlay them on a landscape image.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6Z6t9ov1t3fbbQojbYbGDh/961cef0f06780bfd8c088772a7add796/image11.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-2928 16\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;959\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sub&gt;Photographs by &lt;/sub&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.pexels.com/@guyjoben/\&quot;&gt;&lt;sub&gt;&lt;u&gt;Guy Hurst&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;sub&gt; (landscape), &lt;/sub&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.pexels.com/@oskar-gackowski-2150870625/\&quot;&gt;&lt;sub&gt;&lt;u&gt;Oskar Gackowski&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;sub&gt; (ice cream), and me (dog)&lt;/sub&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Here is a snippet that you can use to overlay images in a worker:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Javascript\&quot;&gt;export default {\n  async fetch(request,env) {\n    const baseURL = &amp;quot;{image-url}&amp;quot;;\n    const overlayURL = &amp;quot;{image-url}&amp;quot;;\n    \n    // Fetch responses from image URLs\n    const [base, overlay] = await Promise.all([fetch(baseURL),fetch(overlayURL)]);\n\n    return (\n      await env.IMAGES\n        .input(base.body)\n        .draw(\n          env.IMAGES.input(overlay.body)\n            .transform({segment: &amp;quot;foreground&amp;quot;}), // Optimize the overlay image\n            {top: 0} // Position the overlay\n        )\n        .output({format:&amp;quot;image/webp&amp;quot;})\n    ).response();\n  }\n};&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Background removal is another step in our ongoing effort to enable developers to build interactive and imaginative products. These features are an iterative process, and weâll continue to refine our approach even further. Weâre looking forward to sharing our progress with you.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Read more about applying background removal in our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/transform-images/transform-via-url/#segment\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;documentation&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;appendix-1-inference-time-in-milliseconds\&quot;&gt;Appendix 1: Inference Time in Milliseconds&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    \n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;23-gb-vram-gpu\&quot;&gt;23 GB     &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 an open project by Microsoft, and Cloudflare AutoRAG, conversational search is now a one-click setup for your website.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Publishers and content creators have historically relied on traditional keyword-based search to help users navigate their websiteâs content. However, traditional search is built on outdated assumptions: users type in keywords to indicate intent, and the site returns a list of links for the most relevant results. Itâs up to the visitor to click around, skim pages, and piece together the answer theyâre looking for.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-artificial-intelligence/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; has reset expectations and that paradigm is breaking: how we search for information has fundamentally changed.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;your-new-type-of-visitors\&quot;&gt;Your New Type of Visitors&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#your-new-type-of-visitors\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Users no longer want to search websites the old way. Theyâre used to interacting with AI systems like Copilot, Claude, and ChatGPT, where they can simply ask a question and get an answer. Weâve moved from search engines to answer engines.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At the same time, websites now have a new class of visitors, AI agents. Agents face the same pain with keyword search: they have to issue keyword queries, click through links, and scrape pages to piece together answers. But they also need more: a structured way to ask questions and get reliable answers across websites. This means that websites need a way to give the agents they trust controlled access, so that information is retrieved accurately.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Website owners need a way to participate in this shift.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;a-new-search-model-for-the-agentic-web\&quot;&gt;A New Search Model for the Agentic Web&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#a-new-search-model-for-the-agentic-web\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;If AI has reset expectations, what comes next? To meet both people and agents where they are, websites need more than incremental upgrades to keyword search. They need a model that makes conversational access to content a first-class part of the web itself.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Thatâs what we want to deliver: combining an open standard (NLWeb) with the infrastructure (AutoRAG) to make it simple for any website to become AI-ready.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://news.microsoft.com/source/features/company-news/introducing-nlweb-bringing-conversational-interfaces-directly-to-the-web/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;NLWeb&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is an open project developed by Microsoft that defines a standard protocol for natural-language queries on websites. Each NLWeb instance also operates as a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server. Cloudflare is building to this spec and actively working with Microsoft to extend the standard with the goal to let every site function like an AI app, so users and agents alike can query its contents naturally.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/autorag/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AutoRAG&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, Cloudflareâs managed retrieval engine, can automatically crawl your website, store the content in R2, and embed it into a managed vector database. AutoRAG keeps the index fresh with continuous re-crawling and re-indexing. Model inference and embedding can be served through Workers AI. Each AutoRAG is paired with an AI Gateway that can provide observability and insights into your AI model usage. This gives you a complete, managed pipeline for conversational search without the burden of managing custom infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;âTogether, NLWeb and AutoRAG let publishers go beyond search boxes, making conversational interfaces for websites simple to create and deploy. This integration will enable every website to easily become AI-ready for both people and trusted agents.â&lt;/i&gt; â R.V. Guha, creator of NLWeb, CVP and Technical Fellow at Microsoft.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;We are optimistic this will open up new monetization models for publishers:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;&amp;quot;The challenges publishers have faced are well known, as are the risks of AI accelerating the collapse of already challenged business models. However, with NLWeb and AutoRAG, there is an opportunity to reset the nature of relationships with audiences for the better. More direct engagement on Publisher Owned and Operated (O&amp;amp;O) environments, where audiences value the brand and voice of the Publisher, means new potential for monetization. This would be the reset the entire industry needs.&amp;quot;&lt;/i&gt;Â  â Joe Marchese, General &amp;amp; Build Partner at Human Ventures.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/blockquote&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;one-click-to-make-your-site-conversational\&quot;&gt;One-Click to Make Your Site Conversational&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#one-click-to-make-your-site-conversational\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;By combining NLWeb&amp;#39;s standard with Cloudflareâs AutoRAG infrastructure, weâre making it possible toÂ  easily bring conversational search to any website.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Simply select your domain in AutoRAG, and it will crawl and index your site for semantic querying. It then deploys a Cloudflare Worker, which acts as the access layer. This Worker implements the NLWeb standard and UI defined by the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/nlweb-ai/NLWeb\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;NLWeb project&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and exposes your indexed content to both people and AI agents.\n\nThe Worker includes:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;`/ask` endpoint:&lt;/b&gt; The defined standard for how conversational web searches should be served. Powers the conversational UI at the root `/` as well as the embeddable preview at `/snippet.html`. It supports chat history so queries can build on one another within the same session, and includes automatic query decontextualization to improve retrieval quality.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;`/mcp` endpoint: &lt;/b&gt;Implements an MCP server that trusted AI agents can connect to for structured access.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;With this setup, your site content is immediately available in two ways for you to experiment: through a conversational UI that you can serve to your visitors, and through a structured MCP interface that lets trusted agents query your site reliably on your terms.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Additionally, if you prefer to deploy and host your own version of the NLWeb project, thereâs also the option to use AutoRAG as the retrieval engine powering the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/nlweb-ai/NLWeb/blob/main/docs/setup-cloudflare-autorag.md\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;NLWeb instance&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1SM7rSQDhoR4fH5KgAJPD7/2266dc2e3c80f3fcc7f17014eb1d0cf1/image5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1496\&quot; height=\&quot;890\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;how-your-site-becomes-conversational\&quot;&gt;How Your Site Becomes Conversational&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-your-site-becomes-conversational\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    \n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/xkeREv3GwXwBZw52Dg6XQ/caeb587819d08eff53a33aa893032b78/image2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;695\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;From your perspective, making your site conversational is just a single click. Behind the scenes, AutoRAG spins up a full retrieval pipeline to make that possible:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Crawling and ingestion: &lt;/b&gt;AutoRAG explores your site like a search engine, following `sitemap.xml` and `robots.txt` files to understand what pages are available and allowed for crawling. From there, it follows your sitemap to discover pages within your domain (up to 100k pages). &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Browser Rendering&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is used to load each page so that it can capture dynamic, JavaScript content. Crawled pages are downloaded into an &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;R2 bucket&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in your account before being ingested.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Continuous Indexing:&lt;/b&gt; Once ingested, the content is parsed and embedded into &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/vectorize/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Vectorize&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, making it queryable beyond keyword matching through semantic search. AutoRAG automatically re-crawls and re-indexes to keep your knowledge base aligned with your latest content.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Access &amp;amp; Observability: &lt;/b&gt;A Cloudflare Worker is deployed in your account to serve as the access layer that implements the NLWeb protocol (you can also find the deployable Worker in the Workers &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/templates\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;templates repository&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;). Workers AI is used to seamlessly power the summarization and decontextualized query capabilities to improve responses. &lt;i&gt;Soon, with the&lt;/i&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;http://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway-aug-2025-refresh/\&quot;&gt;&lt;i&gt;&lt;u&gt; AI Gateway and Secret Store BYO keys&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;i&gt;, youâll be able to connect models from any provider and select them directly in the AutoRAG dashboard.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;road-to-making-websites-a-first-class-data-source\&quot;&gt;Road to Making Websites a First-Class Data Source&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#road-to-making-websites-a-first-class-data-source\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Until now, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/autorag/concepts/how-autorag-works/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AutoRAG&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; only supported R2 as a data source. That worked well for structured files, but we needed to make a website itself a first-class data source to be indexed and searchable. Making that possible meant building website crawling into AutoRAG and strengthening the system to handle large, dynamic sources like websites.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5ouTCcbipVX3s1fPgg6hEs/541a03efb4365370fee5df67cd68841f/image4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1000\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Before implementing our web crawler, we needed to improve the reliability of data syncs. Prior users of AutoRAG lacked visibility into when indexing syncs ran and whether they were successful. To fix this, we introduced a Job module to track all syncs, store history, and provide logs. This required two new Durable Objects to be added into AutoRAGâs architecture:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;JobManager&lt;/b&gt; runs a complete sync, and its duties include queuing files, embedding content, and keeping the Vectorize database up to date.Â  To ensure data consistency, only one JobManager can run per RAG at a time, enforced by the RagManager (a Durable Object in our existing architecture), which cancels any running jobs before starting new ones which can be triggered either manually or by a scheduled sync.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;FileManager&lt;/b&gt; solved scalability issues we hit when Workers ran out of memory during parallel processing. Originally, a single Durable Object was responsible for handling multiple files, but with a 128MB memory limit it quickly became a bottleneck. The solution was to break the work apart: JobManager now distributes files across many FileManagers, each responsible for a single file. By processing 20 files in parallel through 20 different FileManagers, we expanded effective memory capacity from 128MB to roughly 2.5GB per batch.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;With these improvements, we were ready to build the website parser. By reusing our existing R2-based queuing logic, we added crawling with minimal disruption:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;A JobManager designated for a website crawl begins by reading the sitemaps associated with the RAG configuration.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Instead of listing objects from an R2 bucket, it queues each website link into our existing R2-based queue, using the full URL as the R2 object key.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;From here, the process is nearly identical to our file-based sync. A FileManager picks up the job and checks if the RAG is configured for website parsing.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;If it is, the FileManager crawls the link and places the page&amp;#39;s HTML contents into the user&amp;#39;s R2 bucket, again using the URL as the object key.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;After these steps, we index the data and serve it at query time. This approach maximized code reuse, and any improvements to our HTML-to-Markdown conversion now benefit both file and website-based RAGs automatically.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;get-started-today\&quot;&gt;Get Started Today&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#get-started-today\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Getting your website ready for conversational search through NLWeb and AutoRAG is simple. Hereâs how:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;In the &lt;b&gt;Cloudflare Dashboard&lt;/b&gt;, navigate to &lt;b&gt;Compute &amp;amp; AI &amp;gt; AutoRAG&lt;/b&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Select &lt;b&gt;Create&lt;/b&gt; in AutoRAG, then choose the &lt;b&gt;NLWeb Website&lt;/b&gt; quick deploy option.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Select the &lt;b&gt;domain&lt;/b&gt; from your Cloudflare account that you want indexed.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Click &lt;b&gt;Start indexing&lt;/b&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;Thatâs it! You can now try out your NLWeb search experience via the provided link, and test out how it will look on your site by using the embeddable snippet.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1298\&quot; height=\&quot;873\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâd love to hear your feedback as you experiment Some bots are evidently malicious â such as mass price scrapers or those testing stolen credit cards. Others are helpful, like the bots that index your website. Cloudflare has segmented this second category of helpful bot traffic through our <a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/#verified-bots"><u>verified bots</u></a> program, <a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/verified-bots/policy/"><u>vetting</u></a> and validating bots that are transparent about who they are and what they do.</p><p>Today, the rise of <a href="https://agents.cloudflare.com/"><u>agents</u></a> has transformed how we interact with the Internet, often blurring the distinctions between benign and malicious bot actors. Bots are no longer directed only by the bot owners, but also by individual end users to act on their behalf. These bots directed by end users are often working in ways that website owners want to allow, such as planning a trip, ordering food, or making a purchase.</p><p>Our customers have asked us for easier, more granular ways to ensure specific <a href="https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/bots/what-is-a-bot/"><u>bots</u></a>, <a href="https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/bots/what-is-a-web-crawler/"><u>crawlers</u></a>, and <a href="https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-agentic-ai/"><u>agents</u></a> can reach their websites, while continuing to block bad actors. Thatâs why weâre excited to introduce <b>signed agents</b>, an extension of our verified bots program that gives a new bot classification in our security rules and in Radar. Cloudflare has long recognized agents â but weâre now endowing them with their own classification to make it even easier for our customers to set the traffic lanes they want for their website.Â </p>
    <div class="flex anchor relative">
      <h2 id="the-age-of-agents">The age of agents</h2>
      <a href="#the-age-of-agents" aria-hidden="true" class="relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5">
        <svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><path fill="currentcolor" d="m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z"></path></svg>
      </a>
    </div>
    <p>Cloudflare has continuously expanded our verified bot categorization to include different functions as the market has evolved. For instance, we first announced our grouping of <a href="https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-bots/"><u>AI crawler traffic as an official bot category</u></a> in 2023. And in 2024, when OpenAI announced a <a href="https://openai.com/index/searchgpt-prototype/"><u>new AI search prototype</u></a> and introduced <a href="https://platform.openai.com/docs/bots"><u>three different bots</u></a> with distinct purposes, we <a href="https://blog.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-ai-audit-control-ai-content-crawlers/"><u>added three new categories</u></a> to account for this innovation: AI Search, AI Assistant, and Archiver.</p><p>But the bot landscape is constantly evolving. Let&#39;s unpack a common type of verified AI bot â an AI crawler such as <a href="https://radar.cloudflare.com/bots/directory/gptbot"><u>GPTBot</u></a>. Even though the bot performs an array of tasks, the botâs ultimate purpose is a singular, repetitive task on behalf of the operator of that bot: fetch and index information. Its intelligence is applied to performing that singular job on behalf of that bot owner.Â </p><p>Agents, though, are different. Think about an AI agent tasked by a user to &quot;Book the best deal for a round-trip flight to New York City next month.&quot; These agents sometimes use remote browsing products like Cloudflare&#39;s <a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/"><u>Browser Rendering</u></a> and similar products from companies like Browserbase and Anchor Browser. And here is the key distinction: this particular type of bot isnât operating on behalf of a single company, like OpenAI in the prior example, but rather the end users themselves.Â </p>
    <div class="flex anchor relative">
      <h2 id="introducing-signed-agents">Introducing signed agents</h2>
      <a href="#introducing-signed-agents" aria-hidden="true" class="relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5">
        <svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><path fill="currentcolor" d="m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z"></path></svg>
      </a>
    </div>
    <p>In May, we announced Web Bot Auth, a new method of <a href="https://blog.cloudflare.com/web-bot-auth/"><u>using cryptography to verify bot and agent traffic</u></a>. HTTP message signatures allow bots to authenticate themselves and allow customer origins to identify them. This is one of the authentication methods we use today for our verified bots program.Â </p><p>What, exactly, is a <a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/signed-agents/"><u>signed agent</u></a>? First, they are agents that are generally directed by an end user instead of a single company or entity. Second, the infrastructure or remote browsing platform the agents use is signing their HTTP requests via Web Both Auth, with Cloudflare validating these message signatures. And last, they comply with our <a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/signed-agents/policy/"><u>signed agent policy</u></a>.</p><p>The signed agents classification improves on our existing frameworks in a couple of ways:</p><ol><li><p><b>Increased precision and visibility:</b> weâve updated the <i>Cloudflare bots and agents directory to include signed agents</i> in addition to verified bots. This allows us to verify the cryptographic signatures of a much wider set of automated traffic, and our customers to granularly apply their security preferences more easily. Bot operators can now <i>submit signed agent applications from the Cloudflare dashboard</i>, allowing bot owners to specify to us how they think we should segment their automated traffic.Â </p></li><li><p><b>Easier controls from security rules</b>: similar to how they can take action on verified bots as a group, our Enterprise customers will be able to take action on <i>signed agents as a group when configuring their security rules</i>. This new field will be available in the Cloudflare dashboard under security rules soon.</p></li></ol><p>To apply to have an agent added to Cloudflareâs directory of bots and agents, customers should complete the <a href="https://dash.cloudflare.com?to=/:account/configurations/bot-submission-form"><u>Bot Submission Form</u></a> in the Cloudflare dashboard. Here, they can specify whether the submission should be considered for the signed agents list or the verified bots list. All signed agents will be recognized by their cryptographic signatures through <a href="https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-meunier-web-bot-auth-architecture"><u>Web Bot Auth validation</u></a>.Â </p>
          <figure class="kg-card kg-image-card">
          <Image src="https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5caeGdhlmI3dO3GNZKeEUg/0dac239a94732404861b3876f6bdb8b6/BLOG-2930_2.png" alt="BLOG-2930 2" class="kg-image" width="1884" height="1520" loading="lazy"/>
          </figure><p><sub>The Bot Submission Form, available in the Cloudflare dashboard for bot owners to submit both verified bot and signed agent applications.</sub></p><p>We want to be clear: our verified bots program isnât going anywhere. In fact, well-behaved and transparent applications that make use of signed agents can further qualify to be a verified bot, if their specific service adheres to our <a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/verified-bots/policy/"><u>policy</u></a>. For instance,<a href="https://radar.cloudflare.com/scan"> <u>Cloudflare Radar&#39;s URL Scanner</u></a>, which relies on Browser Rendering as a service to scan URLs, is a <a href="https://radar.cloudflare.com/bots/directory/cloudflare-radar-url-scanner"><u>verified bot</u></a>. While Browser Rendering itself does not qualify to be a verified bot, URL Scanner does, since the bot owner (in this case, Cloudflare Radar) directs the traffic sent by the bot and always identifies itself with a unique Web Bot Auth signature â distinct from <a href="https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/reference/automatic-request-headers/"><u>Browser Renderingâs signature</u></a>.Â </p>
    <div class="flex anchor relative">
      <h2 id="from-an-agents-perspective">From an agentâs perspectiveâ¦Â </h2>
      <a href="#from-an-agents-perspective" aria-hidden="true" class="relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5">
        <svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><path fill="currentcolor" d="m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z"></path></svg>
      </a>
    </div>
    <p>Since the launch of Web Bot Auth, our own Browser Rendering product has been sending signed Web Bot Auth HTTP headers, and is always given a bot score of 1 for our Bot Management customers. As of today, Browser Rendering will now show up in this new signed agent category.Â </p>
          <figure class="kg-card kg-image-card">
          <Image src="https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1F8Z0E6WqJTxLf9G3PLB3a/84e80539be402066fe02ab60c431100a/BLOG-2930_3.png" alt="BLOG-2930 3" class="kg-image" width="1199" height="303" loading="lazy"/>
          </figure><p>Weâre also excited to announce the first cohort of agents that weâre partnering with and will be classifying as signed agents: <a href="https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-agent/"><u>ChatGPT agent</u></a>, <a href="https://block.xyz/inside/block-open-source-introduces-codename-goose"><u>Goose</u></a> from Block, <a href="https://docs.browserbase.com/introduction/what-is-browserbase"><u>Browserbase</u></a>, and <a href="https://anchorbrowser.io/"><u>Anchor Browser</u></a>. They are perfect examples of this new classification because their remote browsers are used by their end customers, not necessarily the companies themselves. Weâre thrilled to partner with these teams to take this critical step for the AI ecosystem:</p><blockquote><p>â<i>When we built Goose as an open source tool, we designed it to run locally with an extensible architecture that lets developers automate complex workflows. As Goose has evolved to interact with external services and third-party sites on users&#39; behalf, Web Bot Auth enables those sites to trust Goose while preserving what makes it unique. </i><b><i>This authentication breakthrough unlocks entirely new possibilities for autonomous agents</i></b>.&quot; â <b>Douwe Osinga</b>, Staff Software Engineer, Block</p></blockquote><blockquote><p><i>&quot;At Browserbase, we provide web browsing capabilities for some of the largest AI applications. We&#39;re excited to partner with Cloudflare to support the adoption of Web Bot Auth, a critical layer of identity for agents. </i><b><i>For AI to thrive, agents need reliable, responsible web access.</i></b><i>&quot;</i>  â <b>Paul Klein</b>, CEO, Browserbase</p></blockquote><blockquote><p><i>âAnchor Browser has partnered with Cloudflare to let developers ship verified browser agents. This way </i><b><i>trustworthy bots get reliable access while sites stay protected</i></b><i>.â</i> â <b>Idan Raman</b>, CEO, Anchor Browser</p></blockquote>
    <div class="flex anchor relative">
      <h2 id="updated-visibility-on-radar">Updated visibility on Radar</h2>
      <a href="#updated-visibility-on-radar" aria-hidden="true" class="relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5">
        <svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><path fill="currentcolor" d="m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z"></path></svg>
      </a>
    </div>
    <p>We want everyone to be in the know about our bot classifications. Cloudflare began publishing verified bots on our Radar page <a href="https://radar.cloudflare.com/bots#verified-bots"><u>back in 2022</u></a>, meaning anyone on the Internet â Cloudflare customer or not â can see all of our <a href="https://radar.cloudflare.com/bots#verified-bots"><u>verified bots on Radar</u></a>. We dynamically update the list of bots, but show more than just a list: we announced on <a href="https://www.cloudflare.com/en-gb/press-releases/2025/cloudflare-just-changed-how-ai-crawlers-scrape-the-internet-at-large/"><u>Content Independence Day</u></a> that <a href="https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-search-crawl-refer-ratio-on-radar/#one-more-thing"><u>every verified bot would get its own page</u></a> in our public-facing directory on Radar, which includes the traffic patterns that we see for each bot.</p><p>Our directory has been updated to include <a href="https://radar.cloudflare.com/bots/directory"><b><u>both signed agents and verified bots</u></b></a> â we share exactly how Cloudflare classifies the bots that it recognizes, plus we surface all of the traffic that Cloudflare observes from these many recognized agents and bots. Through this updated directory, weâre not only giving better visibility to our customers, but also striving to set a higher standard for transparency of bot traffic on the Internet.Â </p>
          <figure class="kg-card kg-image-card">
          <Image src="https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/65QPFjmbBde3EzHTOwElSL/cccc8f23c37716c251e0c21850855265/BLOG-2930_4.png" alt="BLOG-2930 4" class="kg-image" width="1999" height="741" loading="lazy"/>

    <div class="flex anchor relative">
      <h2 id="whats-now-whats-next">Whatâs now, whatâs next</h2>
      <a href="#whats-now-whats-next" aria-hidden="true" class="relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5">
        <svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><path fill="currentcolor" d="m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z"></path></svg>
      </a>
    </div>
    <p>As of today, the Cloudflare bot directory supports both bots and agents in a more clear-cut way, and customers or agent creators can submit agents to be signed and recognized <a href="https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/configurations/bot-submission-form"><u>through their account dashboard</u></a>. In addition, anyone can see our signed agents and their traffic patterns on Radar. Soon, customers will be able to take action on signed agents as a group within their firewall rules, the same way you can take action on our verified bots.Â </p><p>Agents are changing the way that humans interact with the Internet. Websites need to know what tools are interacting with them, and for the builders of those tools to be able to easily scale. Message signatures help achieve both of these goals, but this is only step one. Cloudflare will continue to make it easier for agents and websites to interact (or not!) at scale, in a seamless way. </p><p>
</p></div></section><section class="post-full-content flex flex-row flex-wrap mw7 center mb4"><div class="post-content lh-copy w-100 gray1 bt b--gray8 pt4">Cloudflare's connectivity cloud protects <a target='_blank' href='https://www.cloudflare.com/network-services/' rel='noreferrer'>entire corporate networks</a>, helps customers build <a target='_blank' href='https://workers.cloudflare.com/' rel='noreferrer'>Internet-scale applications efficiently</a>, accelerates any <a target='_blank' href='https://www.cloudflare.com/performance/accelerate-internet-applications/' rel='noreferrer'>website or Internet application</a>, <a target='_blank' href='https://www.cloudflare.com/ddos/' rel='noreferrer'>wards off DDoS attacks</a>, keeps <a target='_blank' href='https://www.cloudflare.com/application-security/' rel='noreferrer'>hackers at bay</a>, and can help you on <a target='_blank' href='https://www.cloudflare.com/products/zero-trust/' rel='noreferrer'>your journey to Zero Trust</a>.<br/><br/>Visit <a target='_blank' href='https://one.one.one.one/' rel='noreferrer'>1.1.1.1</a> from any device to get started with our free app that makes your Internet faster and safer.<br/><br/>To learn more about our mission to help build a better Internet, <a target='_blank' href='https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/what-is-cloudflare/' rel='noreferrer'>start here</a>. If you&apos;re looking for a new career direction, check out <a target='_blank' href="https://www.cloudflare.com/careers" rel='noreferrer'>our open positions</a>.</div></section><div class="pv2 ph0-l mw7 center" id="social-buttons"><div class="mt5-l mt2 mb4 f2 flex flex-row-ns flex-column flex-wrap"><a id="social-button-hn" title="Discuss on Hacker News" href="https://news.ycombinator.com/submitlink?u=https://blog.cloudflare.com/signed-agents" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="mr2-ns mb0-l white link b pv3 ph3 mb3 " style="background-color:#0055DC"><svg version="1.1" id="Layer_1" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 512 512" class="mr2"><g><path d="M31,31v450h450V31H31z M270.1,287.6v94.9h-28.1v-94.9L165,143.5h31.9L256,254.3l59.1-110.8H347
